### Q48. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Block <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo> </mo></math>of mass m and block <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi></math> are placed on a fixed triangular wedge by means of a massless, inextensible string and a frictionless pulley as shown in the figure. The wedge is inclined at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>45</mn><mo>°</mo></math> to the horizontal on both sides. The coefficient of friction between block A and the wedge is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>3</mn></math> and that between block <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> and the wedge is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>3</mn></math>. If the blocks <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> are released from rest, find the acceleration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0009112_Ques1_1..jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="height: 151px; width: 230px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Acceleration of block A:
Maximum friction force that can be obtained at A is
 
(f<sub>max</sub>)<sub>A</sub> =μ<sub>A</sub>(mg cos 45<sup>0</sup>) = <math><mrow><mfrac linethickness="1"><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mrow></math>(mg /√2) = <math><mrow><mfrac linethickness="1"><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mrow></math>
 <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0009112_AnsExp1_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 366px; height: 222px;" />

Similarly,

(f<sub>max</sub> )<sub>B</sub> = μ<sub>B</sub> (2mg cos 45°) = <math><mrow><mfrac linethickness="1"><mn>1</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mrow><mo maxsize="1.00em">(</mo><mfrac linethickness="1"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo maxsize="1.00em">)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mfrac linethickness="1"><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mrow></math>

Therefore, maximum value of friction that can be obtained on the system is <math><mrow><mfrac linethickness="1"><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mrow></math>

Net pulling force on the system is
 
F = F<sub>1</sub>-F<sub>2</sub> =<math><mrow><mfrac linethickness="1"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac linethickness="1"><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac linethickness="1"><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></math>.... (ii)
 
From Eqs. (i) and (ii), we can see that Net pulling force &lt; f<sub>max</sub>
 
Therefore, the system will not move or the acceleration of block A will be zero.

</details>

### Q49. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Inside a horizontally moving box, an experimenter finds that when an object is placed on a smooth horizontal table and is released, it moves with an acceleration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>. In this box, if <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> body is suspended with a light string, the tension in the string in equilibrium position (with respect to experimenter) will be (Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 123px; top: 37.6667px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> <mo>N</mo> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>
- **(D)** Zero

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> <mo>N</mo> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Acceleration of box = <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
Inside the box forces acting on bob are shown in the figure.

        <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0051107.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 282px; height: 150px;" />

<math> <mrow> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>ma</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </msqrt> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> <mo>N</mo> </mrow> </math>

### Q50. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> block <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> rests as shown on a bracket <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> of the same mass. Constant forces <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math> start to act at a time <math><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> when the distance of block <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> from the pulley is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo> </mo><mi>cm</mi></math>. Time (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>) when block <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> reaches the pulley is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi></math>. Find the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mi>t</mi></math>.

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/cgekcmkozhkldkxlvngqsjtsdiltubnayjqcsvbtxlbmaiouql.jpeg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **5**

**Detailed Solution:**

First, find out the acceleration of A so for this

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>a</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

Now use pseudo concept (in which <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is the non-inertial frame)

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/eplenwwzxlxoiyhcxghhytdzaqhqvwlaqkgjlfssvzgbgbakal.jpeg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" />

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/zcmsppcpenawhqqxupytdiybctrukrefgqadoxelshtcbvdrxk.jpeg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" />

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

Now <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>50</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>100</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>10</mn><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q51. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

When forces <em><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math><mtext>,</mtext><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>,<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math></em> are acting on a particle of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo> </mo></math>such that <em><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></em> and<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo> </mo><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> are mutually perpendicular then the particle remains stationary. If the force <em><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math></em> is now removed then the acceleration of the particle is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <em><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>m</mi></mfrac></math></em><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: -15px; top: -6px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(B)** <em><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math></em><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: -16px; top: -6px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(C)** <em><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi>m</mi></mfrac></math></em><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: -4px; top: -6px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>
- **(D)** <em><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>m</mi></mfrac></math></em><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: 1px; top: -6px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <em><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>m</mi></mfrac></math></em><div id="gtx-trans" style="position: absolute; left: -15px; top: -6px;"><div class="gtx-trans-icon"> </div></div>**

**Detailed Solution:**

According to equilibrium total force acting on a particle remains zero

<math><mover accent="true"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math><mover accent="true"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>→</mo></mover></math>

<math><mo>∴</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q52. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

For the arrangement in the figure, the particle <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></math> attached to one end of a string which moves on a horizontal table in a circle of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>l</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math> (where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>l</mi></math> is the length of the string) with the constant angular speed <math><mi mathvariant="normal">ω</mi></math>. The other end of the string attached to mass <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> which rests on a vertical rod. When the rod collapse, the acceleration of mass <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> at that instant

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image8-ca32010f-92d5-4bd0-a566-791efe2ab259.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="height: 263px; width: 250px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>l</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>l</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>l</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>l</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

From Newton&#39;s second law


<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image9-36f32d3b-300d-4a85-8379-e16c2855451f.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" />

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mfrac><mi>l</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math>

And <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math>

After solving above equations, we get

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>l</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q53. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle is moving in a uniform circular motion on a horizontal surface. Particle position and velocity at time <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> are shown in the figure in the coordinate system. Which of the indicated variable on the vertical axis is incorrectly matched by the graph shown alongside for particle's motion-

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014778_Question_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 185px; height: 152px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** y component of force keeping particle moving in a circle

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014778_Answer_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 201px; height: 142px;" />
- **(B)** x component of velocity

<math> </math><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014778_Answer_3.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 199px; height: 140px;" />
- **(C)** Angular velocity of the particle

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014778_Answer_2.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 199px; height: 141px;" />
- **(D)** x coordinate of the particle

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014778_Answer_4.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 197px; height: 142px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) x component of velocity

<math> </math><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014778_Answer_3.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 199px; height: 140px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/8585415841.JPG" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 200px; height: 208px;" />

where, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>F</mi><mi>cos</mi><mi>θ</mi></math>, and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>F</mi><mi>sin</mi><mi>θ</mi></math>

at time <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi></math>, in Uniform circular motion only centripetal force exists constant in magnitude

So, Y - component of force is <math> <mo>⇒</mo><mover accent="true"> <mrow> <msub><mtext>F</mtext><mtext>y</mtext></msub> </mrow> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mtext> </mtext><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mtext>F</mtext><mtext>sin</mtext><mtext>θ</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mover accent="true"> <mtext>j</mtext> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> </math>

<math> <msub><mtext>F</mtext><mtext>y</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mtext>F</mtext><mtext>sin</mtext><mtext>ω</mtext><mtext>t</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mover accent="true"> <mtext>j</mtext> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover> </math> (as, <!-- MathType@Translator@5@5@MathML2 (namespace attr).tdl@MathML 2.0 (namespace attr)@ --> <math> <mrow> <mtext>θ</mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext>ωt</mtext></mrow> </math> <!-- MathType@End@5@5@ --> )

So given graph is correct for <math><msub><mtext>F</mtext><mtext>y</mtext></msub></math> vs <math><mtext>t</mtext></math>

In Uniform circular motion. <math> <mrow> <mtext>V</mtext><mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mtext>ω</mtext> <mtext>r</mtext> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> </mrow> </math>  constant

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/59654964196.JPG" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 200px; height: 215px;" />

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/416418541.JPG" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 200px; height: 190px;" />

<math> <mo>⇒</mo><mover accent="true"> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mtext>x</mtext> </msub> </mrow> <mo stretchy="true">→</mo> </mover> <mo>=</mo><mo>−</mo><mtext>V</mtext><mtext>sin</mtext><mtext>ω</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext>t</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mover accent="true"> <mtext>j</mtext> <mo stretchy="true">^</mo> </mover></math>

So given graph for <math> <msub> <mtext>V</mtext> <mtext>x</mtext> </msub> <mo>−</mo><mtext>t</mtext> </math> is incorrect.

In Uniform circular motion.,   <math> <mrow> <mtext>ω</mtext><mo>=</mo> </mrow> </math> constant

<math> <mrow> <mo>∴</mo><mtext> </mtext><mtext>ω</mtext><mo>−</mo><mtext>t</mtext> </mrow> </math>   graph is correct

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/8418541796.JPG" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 200px; height: 173px;" />

<math> <mo>∵</mo><mtext> </mtext><mtext>x</mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext>r</mtext><mtext>cos</mtext><mtext>θ</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mo>=</mo><mtext> </mtext><mtext>r</mtext><mtext>cos</mtext><mtext>ω</mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext>t</mtext> </math>

<math> <mo>⇒</mo><mtext> </mtext><mtext>x</mtext><mo>−</mo><mtext>t</mtext> </math>  graph is correct

</details>

### Q54. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Two masses <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></math> are attached with strings as shown in the figure. For the system to be in equilibrium, we must have

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/26516.JPG" style="width: 200px; height: 226px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mtext>tan</mtext> <mi>θ</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>m</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mtext>tan</mtext> <mi>&theta;</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>m</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mtext>tan</mtext> <mi>&theta;</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>m</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mtext>tan</mtext> <mi>&theta;</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>m</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math> <mrow> <mtext>tan</mtext> <mi>θ</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>m</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>mg</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>Tsin</mi><mn>45</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8658;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo> <mtext>mg</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> <mo>T</mo> </mrow> </math>

  <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mtext>&#160;cos</mtext> <mi>&#952;</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>cos</mo> <mn>4</mn> <msup> <mn>5</mn> <mo>&#8728;</mo> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8658;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mtext>cos</mtext> <mi>&#952;</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>T</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>              ...(i)

Further, <math> <mrow> <mtext>Mg</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mo>T</mo> <mtext>&#160;cos&#160;</mtext> <mn>4</mn> <msup> <mn>5</mn> <mo>&#8728;</mo> </msup> <mo>=</mo> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mtext>&#160;sin</mtext> <mi>&#952;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mi> <mfenced close="}" open="{" separators=","> <mrow> <mo>&#8757;</mo> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8658;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo> <msub> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>1</mn> </msub> <mtext>&#160;sin</mtext> <mi>&#952;</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>Mg</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>              ...(ii)

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8658;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo> <mtext>tan</mtext> <mi>&#952;</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>Mg</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>m</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>  {dividing Eq. (ii) by Eq. (i)}

</details>

---

### Q55. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

The coefficient of friction between the two blocks shown in the figure is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>μ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.4</mn></math> and between the lower block and the ground is zero. The blocks are given velocities of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> respectively in the direction shown in the figure. In how much time (in seconds) the slipping between the blocks will stop?


<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image17-96bde677-883d-40ad-b424-719f177fcc14.png" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The common velocity of the blocks can be calculated as follows</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>The acceleration of the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> block is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>t</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q56. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

The mass of a pendulum bob is m = 2 kg and the length of the string is <math><mi>l</mi></math>. It is displaced through <math><mn>90</mn><mo>°</mo></math> from the vertical and released. What minimum tension (in N) should the string should be able to withstand so that it doesn't break during motion?

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **60**

**Detailed Solution:**

Velocity at the lowest point, <math><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>l</mi></msqrt></math>

At the lowest point, the tension in the string

<math><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi> = 60 N </mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q57. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Three blocks are suspended as shown in the figure. The acceleration of the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>500</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> block is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0629600.jpg" style="width: 300px; height: 160px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>13</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> downwards
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>13</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>13</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>13</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> upwards

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>13</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>500</mn><mi>g</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>500</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>(1)</mo></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>100</mn><mi>g</mi><mi>sin</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8242;&#8242;</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mi>a</mi></math>
Or, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8242;&#8242;</mo></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>50</mn><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>(2)</mo></math>
<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0629600_Sol.jpg" style="width: 294px; height: 181px;" />
Again, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>T</mi><mo>&#8242;&#8242;</mo></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>50</mn><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>50</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>(3)</mo></math>

From equations (2) and (3),
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>100</mn><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>150</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>(4)</mo></math></p><p>Adding equations (1) and (4), <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>400</mn><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>650</mn><mi>a</mi></math> or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>400</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow><mn>650</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow><mn>13</mn></mfrac></math>
This acceleration is downwards.</p>

</details>

### Q58. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>The friction coefficient between the board and the floor shown in diagram is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>μ</mi></math>. Find the maximum force that the man can exert on the rope, so that the board does not slip on the floor.</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/6-E722112.jpg" style="width: 230px; height: 183px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mrow>  <mfrac>    <mrow>      <mtext>&mu;</mtext>      <mfenced open="(" close=")" separators=",">        <mrow>          <mtext>m</mtext>          <mo>+</mo>          <mtext>M</mtext>        </mrow>      </mfenced>      <mtext>g</mtext>    </mrow>    <mrow>      <mfenced open="(" close=")" separators=",">        <mrow>          <mn>2</mn>          <mo>+</mo>          <mtext>&mu;</mtext>        </mrow>      </mfenced>    </mrow>  </mfrac></mrow></math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>μ</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>m</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mtext>g</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>μ</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math><mrow>  <mfrac>    <mrow>      <mtext>&mu;</mtext>      <mfenced open="(" close=")" separators=",">        <mrow>          <mtext>m</mtext>          <mo>+</mo>          <mtext>M</mtext>        </mrow>      </mfenced>      <mtext>g</mtext>    </mrow>    <mrow>      <mfenced open="(" close=")" separators=",">        <mrow>          <mn>2</mn>          <mo>-</mo>          <mtext>&mu;</mtext>        </mrow>      </mfenced>    </mrow>  </mfrac></mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math><mrow>  <mfrac>    <mrow>      <mtext>&mu;</mtext>      <mfenced open="(" close=")" separators=",">        <mrow>          <mtext>m</mtext>          <mo>+</mo>          <mtext>M</mtext>        </mrow>      </mfenced>      <mtext>g</mtext>    </mrow>    <mrow>      <mfenced open="(" close=")" separators=",">        <mrow>          <mn>1</mn>          <mo>-</mo>          <mtext>&mu;</mtext>        </mrow>      </mfenced>    </mrow>  </mfrac></mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>μ</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>m</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>M</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mtext>g</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>μ</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The forces acting on the system are shown in the diagram

For vertical equilibrium of the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mi>F</mi></math>                      ....(i)

<u><strong><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014128_AnsExp2_1.jpg" style="width: 200px; height: 169px;" /></strong></u>

And for the vertical equilibrium of the system

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014128_AnsExp2_2.jpg" style="width: 165px; height: 117px;" />

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo> </mo><mo>+</mo><mo> </mo><mi>T</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><mo>+</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>)</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>,  i.e.,  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo> </mo><mi>R</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><mo>+</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mo>)</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo> </mo><mo>-</mo><mo> </mo><mi>T</mi></math>             ....(ii)

Now the system will not move horizontally till

              <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo> </mo><mi>T</mi><mo> </mo><mo>&lt;</mo><mo> </mo><msub><mi>f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></msub></math>

i.e.,        <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo> </mo><mi>T</mi><mo> </mo><mo>&lt;</mo><mo> </mo><mi>μ</mi><mo>[</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><mo>+</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo> </mo><mo>-</mo><mo> </mo><mi>T</mi><mo>]</mo></math>              <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo> </mo><msub><mi>f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi></msub><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mi>μ</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

which on simplification gives

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mi>T</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mfenced><mrow><mtext>m</mtext><mo>+</mo><mtext>M</mtext></mrow></mfenced><mtext>g</mtext></mrow><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>μ</mi></mrow></mfenced></mfrac></math>

So in the light of equation (i), we get

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><msub><mi>F</mi><mtext>max</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>μ</mi><mfenced><mrow><mtext>m</mtext><mo>+</mo><mtext>M</mtext></mrow></mfenced><mtext>g</mtext></mrow><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>μ</mi></mrow></mfenced></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q59. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Two identical ladders are arranged as shown in the figure. Mass of the block is <math><mi>m</mi></math> and the mass of each ladder is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi></math>. The length of each of the ladder is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math>. The system is in equilibrium. What is the magnitude of frictional force acting at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>?

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0009117_Ques1_1.jpg" style="width: 250px; height: 161px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">mg</mtext><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mtext>cos</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">&#952;</mtext></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">Mg</mtext><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mtext>cos</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">&#952;</mtext></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mtext mathvariant="italic">M</mtext><mo>-</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">m</mtext></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">g</mtext><mtext>&#160;cos&#160;</mtext><mtext mathvariant="italic">&#952;</mtext></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mtext mathvariant="italic">M</mtext><mo>+</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">m</mtext></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mtext mathvariant="italic">g</mtext><mtext>cot</mtext><mi>&#952;</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mtext mathvariant="italic">M</mtext><mo>+</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">m</mtext></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mtext mathvariant="italic">g</mtext><mtext>cot</mtext><mi>&#952;</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Drawing force diagrams of the rod, we have

This is the equilibrium of coplanar forces, hence using the equations

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8721;</mo> <msub> <mtext>F</mtext> <mi>x</mi> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> <mtext>,</mtext> <mo>&#8721;</mo> <msub> <mtext>F</mtext> <mi>y</mi> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </math> and
 
Net moment about point O = 0



<u><strong><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0009117_AnsExp1_1.jpg" style="width: 353px; height: 391px;" /></strong></u>


we have the equations,
 
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>N</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>+</mo></math><math><mrow><mfrac linethickness="1"><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></math>... (i)
 
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>N</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>f</mi></math>  ...(ii)

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>+</mo></math><math><mrow><mfrac linethickness="1"><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></math>      ... (iii)
 
and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi><mi>g</mi></math><math><mrow><mfrac linethickness="1"><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cos</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>f</mi><mi>L</mi><mi>sin</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>N</mi><mi>L</mi><mi>cos</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></math>         ... (iv)

Solving these four equations, we have
 
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">&#8289;</mo><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mtext mathvariant="italic">M</mtext><mo>+</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">m</mtext></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mtext mathvariant="italic">g</mtext><mtext>cot</mtext><mi>&#952;</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q60. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A ring of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> sliding on a frictionless vertical rod connected by a block <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo> </mo></math>of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> by the help of a massless string. Then at equilibrium, the value of <math><mi>θ</mi></math> (in degree) is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ddfffff.JPG" style="width: 200px; height: 144px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **60**

**Detailed Solution:**

For ring,

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/29939_solu.JPG" style="width: 200px; height: 177px;" />

From force diagram of ring,

<math><mi>T</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mi>g</mi></math> ......(i)

For block B.

<math><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>T </mi></math>........ (ii)

<math><mi>T</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mi>g</mi></math>

<math><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mi>g</mi></math>

<math><mo>∴</mo></math> <math><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo></math> <math><mi>θ</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mn>60</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

---

### Q61. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A bullet is fired from a gun. The force on the bullet is given by <math><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>600</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mi>t</mi></math> where <math><mi>F</mi></math> is in newton and <math><mi>t</mi></math> in second. The force on the bullet becomes zero as soon as it leaves the barrel. What is the average impulse imparted to the bullet?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mtext>9 Ns</mtext> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>1</mtext><mtext>.8 Ns</mtext></math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mtext>0</mtext><mtext>.9 Ns</mtext> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow>  <mtext>0</mtext><mtext>.3 Ns</mtext> </mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math> <mrow> <mtext>0</mtext><mtext>.9 Ns</mtext> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given, <math><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>600</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mi> </mi><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math><mo>⟹</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>

Impulese <math> <mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><munderover><mo stretchy="false">∫</mo><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow></munderover><mrow><mi>F</mi><mo>⋅</mo><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mrow><munderover><mo stretchy="false">∫</mo><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></munderover><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>600</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mi> </mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mo>[</mo><mn>600</mn><mi> </mi><mi>t</mi><mo>-</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mi> </mi><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><msubsup><mrow><mo>]</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mn>0.9</mn><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>

</details>

### Q62. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A small block of mass <math> <mtext>m</mtext> </math> is released from rest from point <math> <mtext>A</mtext> </math> inside a smooth hemisphere bowl of radius <math> <mtext>R</mtext> </math>, which is fixed on ground such that <math> <mrow> <mtext>OA</mtext></mrow> </math> is horizontal. The ratio <math> <mrow> <mtext>(x)</mtext></mrow> </math> of magnitude of centripetal force and normal reaction on the block at any point <math> <mtext>B</mtext> </math> varies with <math><mi>θ</mi></math> as:

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/image13-05f109c8-035f-43b8-bd1c-910a6870.jpg" style="width: 237px; height: 194px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image14-b0b3cd15-8ffc-442e-a4f5-8425c2eb42de.jpg" />
- **(B)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image15-65b82a1f-ad7e-4452-a724-90752326f52d.jpg" />
- **(C)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image16-16b93712-e277-48fc-9768-88ccebf1dd32.jpg" />
- **(D)** <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image17-31d6e97b-86bd-4872-931b-a1ec360efa9e.jpg" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image14-b0b3cd15-8ffc-442e-a4f5-8425c2eb42de.jpg" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>N</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mi>N</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image18-ff248e38-07e6-4f3a-8491-b32ea2a1c4a3.jpg" />

By energy conservation,

<math><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mi>R</mi><mi> </mi><mi>s</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>θ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mi>N</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mi>R</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>o</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi><mi>N</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> (constant)

<math><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q63. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> is placed on a plank of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi> </mi><mi>kg</mi><mo>,</mo></math> which is placed on a smooth horizontal plane, as shown in the figure. The coefficient of friction between the block and the plank is <math><mi>μ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> . If a horizontal force <math><mi>F</mi></math> is applied on the plank, then the maximum value of <math><mi>F</mi></math> for which the block and the plank move together is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>/</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/0381976_ques_1.jpg" style="width: 240px; height: 67px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>120</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>F</mi><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></math> assuming no slipping between<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi></math> and <math><mi>M</mi></math></p><p>This means friction between the block and plank is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8804;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8804;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>g</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q64. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

The rear side of a truck is open and a box of mass <math><mn>2</mn><mn> k</mn><mn>g</mn></math> is placed on the truck <math><mn>8</mn><mn> m</mn></math> away from the open end. The truck starts from rest with an acceleration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mn> m</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mn>s</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> on a straight road. The box will fall off the truck when it is at distance from the starting point equal to (in meter)

(Given, <math><mi>μ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.1</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mn> m</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mn>s</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> ).

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **16**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let <math><mi>a</mi></math> be the acceleration of truck,

<img height="88" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/jhjsvnjknyvzqsbrtjmsyaredjogdvcklqgxfbulbdrifdzmfl.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" width="268" />

For block,

<img height="87" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/wtteaqdgpamjqjisgudlonfjciidaaceiehevbdyiatqjqploq.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" width="163" />

<math><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>μ</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><msub><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></math>

<math><msub><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>μ</mi><mi>g</mi></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0.1</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mrow><mrow><mn> m</mn></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>s</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mn>8</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><msub><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mo>⇒</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mn> s</mn></math>

The block will fall off the truck after <math><mn>4</mn><mn> s</mn><mo>.</mo></math>

For truck,

Distance travelled by the truck in this time.

<math><mi>s</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>a</mi><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>16</mn><mi> </mi><mn> m</mn></math>

</details>

### Q65. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A <math> <mrow> <mn>0.5</mn><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext>kg</mtext></mrow> </math> ball moving with a speed of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><msup><mtext> m s</mtext><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> strikes a hard wall at an angle of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo>°</mo></math> with the wall. It is reflected with the same speed at the same angle. If the ball is in contact with the wall for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0.25</mn><mo> </mo><mo>s</mo></math>, the average force acting on the wall is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi>96 N</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi>48 N</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi>24 N</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi>12 N</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mi>24 N</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Here, <math> <mrow> <mi>F</mi><mo>×</mo><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo></mrow> </math> change in momentum

<math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>m</mi><mi>v</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi>sin</mi><mi>θ</mi></mrow> </math>

     <img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0017449_AnsExp_1-.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 105px; height: 200px;" />

<math> <mrow> <mo>∴</mo> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mi>F</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2 </mn> <mi>m</mi><mi>v</mi> <mtext> sin</mtext> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>t</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

              <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mo>×</mo> <mtext>0.5</mtext> <mo>×</mo> <mtext>12</mtext> <mo>×</mo> <mtext>sin</mtext> <msup> <mtext>30</mtext> <mtext>o</mtext> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>0.25</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>48</mtext> <mo>×</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>24 N</mtext> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q66. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In the figure, the mass of a ball is <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> times the mass of the rod. Length of the rod is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>. The level of ball is the same as rod level. Find out the time taken by the ball to reach at the upper end of the rod.

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/fofctxednyiabhvlmqybkxyervqgfxqpomeotudesrdkjukyqb.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.4</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.45</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.25</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>5</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.4</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let <math><msub><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mi>&#160;</mi></math> and <math><mi>&#160;</mi><msub><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> be accelerations of a ball (upward) and rod (downward), respectively.

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ldegqqbddxvnxiqqhslqajdhxuveuqcpeavtxzekfqbuuxrktx.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" />

Clearly, from the diagram

<math><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> ...(i)

Now, for the ball

<math><mn>2</mn><mi>T</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi></math> ...(ii)

and for the rod, <math><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><msub><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> ...(iii)

On solving equations (i) and (iii), we get

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>a</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>g</mi><mn>29</mn></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8593;</mo></math> (upward)

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow><mn>29</mn></mfrac><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#8595;</mo></math> (downward)

So, acceleration of ball w.r.t rod <math><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>29</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

Now, displacement of ball w.r.t. rod when it reaches the upper end of rod is <math><mn>1</mn><mi>m</mi></math> .

Using the equation of motion,

<math><mi>s</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>u</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>a</mi><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mi>s</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>29</mn></mrow></mfrac><msup><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mn>58</mn><mn>30</mn></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.4</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

</details>

---

### Q67. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In figure, a particle is placed at the highest point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> of a smooth sphere of radius <math><mi>r</mi></math>. It is given slight push, and it leaves the sphere at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>, at a depth <math><mi>h</mi><mi> </mi></math>vertically below <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>. The value of <math><mi>h</mi></math> is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/48519_que.JPG" style="width: 150px; height: 169px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>r</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>r</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>r</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>r</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

If <math><mi>v</mi></math> velocity acquired at <math><mi>B</mi></math>, then

<math><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>h</mi></math>

The particle will leave the sphere at <math><mi>B</mi></math>, when

<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>mv</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>r</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>mgcos</mtext> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>gh</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>r</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>g</mtext> <mtext>.</mtext> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>r</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>h</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>r</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/WhatsApp_Image_2020-07-02_at_3.16.37_PM.jpeg" style="width: 200px; height: 184px;" />

Which gives <math><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q68. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A horizontal force <math><mi>F</mi></math> is applied at the top of an equilateral triangular block having mass <math><mi>m</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The minimum coefficient of friction required to topple the block before translation will be

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/cjeojglikbmefxdjgldtmshjackstbtyxbyfucyplfyyfbqwcn.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** None of these

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>⇒</mo><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math> 

         <math><mo>⇒</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>×</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>a</mi><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>⇒</mo><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math> 

         <math><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow></msub><mo>⇒</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mi>μ</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>⇒</mo><mi>μ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math> 

         <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/fxwdkvrxzdgzjfprjtxmcdgtzcowlgavwqssuonwqwgpsigsro.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain />

</details>

### Q69. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Masses, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> are connected by strings of negligible mass which pass over massless and frictionless pulleys <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math><span style="font-size: 10.8333px;"> </span>and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> as shown in the figure. The masses move such that the portion of the string between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is parallel to the inclined plane and the portion of the string between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is horizontal. The masses <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> each and the coefficient of kinetic friction between the masses and the surfaces is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math>. The inclined plane makes an angle of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>37</mn><mo>°</mo></math> with the horizontal and the mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> moves downwards with a uniform velocity. Find the tension in the horizontal portion of the string.
(Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>sin</mi><mo> </mo><mn>37</mn><mo>°</mo></math><math> <mrow> <mo>≅</mo> </mrow> </math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac bevelled="true"><mn>3</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math>)</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0009102_Ques1_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 327px; height: 224px;" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **9.8**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Constant velocity means the net acceleration of the system is zero. Or the net pulling force on the system is zero. While calculating the pulling force, tension forces are not taken into consideration. Therefore,

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0009102_AnsExp1_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 250px; height: 124px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo> </mo><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>g</mi><mo> </mo><mi>sin</mi><mo> </mo><mn>37</mn><mo>°</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>μ</mi><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>g</mi><mo> </mo><mi>cos</mi><mn>37</mn><mo>°</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>μ</mi><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi>g</mi></math>

or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo> </mo><mi>sin</mi><mo> </mo><mn>37</mn><mo>°</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>μ</mi><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo> </mo><mi>cos</mi><mo> </mo><mn>37</mn><mo>°</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>μ</mi><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
 
Substituting the values
 
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac linethickness="1"><mn>3</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mn>0.25</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac linethickness="1"><mn>4</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mn>0.25</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo> </mo><mo>  </mo><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.2</mo><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math>

Since, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is moving with uniform velocity</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>μ</mi><msub><mi>M</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>)</mo><mo> </mo><mo>(</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo><mo> </mo><mo>(</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q70. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The pulleys and strings shown in the figure are smooth and of negligible mass. For the system to remain in equilibrium, the angle <math><mi>θ</mi> </math> should be

<img alt="" height="156" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/IL0000445_questi_1.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" width="246" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mn>45</mn><mo>°</mo> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mn>60</mn><mo>°</mo> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mn>0</mn><mo>°</mo> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mn>30</mn><mo>°</mo> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math> <mrow> <mn>45</mn><mo>°</mo> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The tension in both the strings will be same.<p> </p><img alt="" height="219" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/IL0000445_sol_3_1.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" width="300" /><p> </p>For equilibrium in vertical direction for body <i><span>B </span></i><span>we have

<math> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> <mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>T</mi><mi>cos</mi><mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>∴</mo><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> <mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mi>cos</mi><mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </math>

( <math> <mrow> <mo>∵</mo><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi> </mrow> </math> </span><span>at equilibrium)

<math> <mrow> <mo>∴</mo><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mi>cos</mi><mi>θ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> </mfrac> <mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mo>⇒</mo><mi>θ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>45</mn><mo>°</mo> </mrow> </math> </span>

</details>
