### Q71. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A dynamometer <math><mi>D</mi></math>, which is a device used to measure force, is attached to two blocks of masses <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math>. Forces of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math> are applied on the blocks as shown in the figure. The reading of the dynamometer is

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/0046233.PNG" style="width: 500px; height: 75px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>14</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>14</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Make a free body diagram then write force equation for the blocks involving spring force.

<math><mn>20</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>a</mi></math>

<math><mi>T</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>a</mi></math>

<math><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> and <math><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>14</mn></math> N

</details>

### Q72. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

Coefficient of kinetic friction between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> block is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn></math>. The horizontal table surface is smooth. Find the acceleration (in S.I unit) of the block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math>.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/425308_squeee.png" style="width: 300px; height: 173px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>a</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>a</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi></math></p><p>Adding the above three equations and solving for acceleration we get</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>100</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0.25</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0.25</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>20</mn></mrow><mn>15</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q73. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

An aircraft loops a vertical loop of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo> </mo><mo>=</mo><mo> </mo><mn>500</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> with a constant velocity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>360</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi>kmh</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. Find the weight of the flier of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>70</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> in the lower, upper, and middle points of the loop.

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi></math>
- **(D)** none of these

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#xA0;</mo><mi>kN</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Forces on the flier in the frame of aircaraft (non inertial reference frame) is:

(i) Normal reaction (N)

(ii)Pseudo force <math> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>m</mi> <msup> <mrow> <mi>v</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

(iii) Gravitational force d(mg)

According to problem,

                        v<sub>T</sub> = v<sub>b</sub> = v<sub>m</sub> = v

The net force on flier at every point should be zero in the frame of aircraft. For top point T,

                             <math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi>N</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>T</mi> </mrow> </msub> <mo>+</mo> <mtext>mg</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msubsup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>mv</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mtext>T</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </mfrac> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mo>∴</mo> <msub> <mrow> <mi>N</mi> </mrow> <mrow><mi>T</mi> </mrow> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mi>mv</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> </math>

                             = 0.7 kN

For bottom point,<math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>B</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>mg</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msubsup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>mv</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mi>b</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mtext>mg</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>mv</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mi></mi><mi>R</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

                             = 2.1 kN.

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014518_AnsExp_1_1.jpg" style="width: 214px; height: 219px;" />

For middle point,<math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mi>N</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>m</mi> </mrow> </msub> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msubsup> <mtext>mv</mtext> <mrow> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msubsup> </mrow> <mrow> <mi>R</mi> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>+</mo> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </msqrt> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mtext>.</mtext> <mn>5</mn> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mi>k</mi> <mi></mi><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo> </mrow> </math>

</details>

---

### Q74. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

Two blocks <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> of equal masses are released from an inclined plane of inclination <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>45</mn><mo>°</mo></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>. Both the blocks are initially at rest. The coefficient of kinetic friction between the block <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and the inclined plane is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></math> while it is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn></math> for block <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>. Initially the block <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> behind the block <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>. At what time in seconds will their front faces come in a line, (Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>)

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0009116_Ques1_1.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 250px; height: 181px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

Acceleration of A down the plane,
 
a<sub>A</sub> = g sin 45° -μ<sub>A</sub> g cos 45<sup>o</sup>
 
<math><mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo maxsize="1.00em">(</mo><mn>10</mn><mo maxsize="1.00em">)</mo></mrow><mrow><mo maxsize="1.00em">(</mo><mfrac linethickness="1"><mn>1</mn><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo maxsize="1.00em">)</mo></mrow><mo>-</mo><mrow><mo maxsize="1.00em">(</mo><mn>0.2</mn><mo maxsize="1.00em">)</mo></mrow><mrow><mo maxsize="1.00em">(</mo><mn>10</mn><mo maxsize="1.00em">)</mo></mrow><mrow><mo maxsize="1.00em">(</mo><mfrac linethickness="1"><mn>1</mn><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo maxsize="1.00em">)</mo></mrow></mrow></math>
 
= 4√2m/s<sup>2</sup>
Similarly acceleration of B down the plane, a<sub>B</sub> =g (sin 45°- μ<sub>B</sub> cos 45°)
 
<math><mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo maxsize="1.00em">(</mo><mn>10</mn><mo maxsize="1.00em">)</mo></mrow><mrow><mo maxsize="1.00em">(</mo><mfrac linethickness="1"><mn>1</mn><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo maxsize="1.00em">)</mo></mrow><mo>-</mo><mrow><mo maxsize="1.00em">(</mo><mn>0.3</mn><mo maxsize="1.00em">)</mo></mrow><mrow><mo maxsize="1.00em">(</mo><mn>10</mn><mo maxsize="1.00em">)</mo></mrow><mrow><mo maxsize="1.00em">(</mo><mfrac linethickness="1"><mn>1</mn><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo maxsize="1.00em">)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>3.5</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mfrac linethickness="1"><mi>m</mi><mrow><msup><mi>s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></mrow></math>
 
The front face of A and B will come in a line when,
 
s<sub>a</sub> = s<sub>B</sub> + √2

or <math><mrow><mfrac linethickness="1"><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>u</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac linethickness="1"><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>a</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></math>
 
<math><mrow><mfrac linethickness="1"><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>4</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>×</mo><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac linethickness="1"><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>3.5</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>×</mo><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></math>

Solving this equation, we get t = 2 s

Additional information, <math><mrow><msub><mi>s</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac linethickness="1"><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>a</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac linethickness="1"><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>4</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mo maxsize="1.00em">(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo maxsize="1.00em">)</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn></mn><mn>8</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi>m</mi></mrow></math>
 
So, both the blocks will come in a line after A has travelled a distance 8√2 m down the plane.

</details>

---

### Q75. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Water from a hosepipe of radius <math><mn>5</mn><mi> </mi><mi>cm</mi></math> strikes a wall at a speed of  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> normally and stops. The force exerted on the wall in newton is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>13.5</mn><mn>π</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>6.25</mn><mi>π</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>62.5</mn><mi>π</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>27</mn><mi>π</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mn>62.5</mn><mi>π</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given, Radius of hose pipe <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#160;cm</mi></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>&#160;m</mi></math>

Speed of water <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#160;m&#160;</mi><msup><mrow><mn>s</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

We know that, <math><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>A</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>&#961;</mi></math>

<math><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>&#961;</mi></math>

<math><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn></math>

<math><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>62.5</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q76. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Water coming with velocity <math><mi>v</mi></math> drops onto a mesh through which <math><mn>50</mn><mi>%</mi><mi> </mi></math> of the water passes by, <math><mn>25</mn><mi>%</mi></math> loses its entire momentum and <math><mn>25</mn><mi>%</mi></math> bounces back. The pressure on the mesh is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>ρ</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>ρ</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>3</mn><mi>ρ</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>ρ</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>ρ</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Mass per unit time <math><mo>=</mo><mi>ρ</mi><mi>A</mi><mi>v</mi></math> 

Force due to momentum loss  <math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>ρ</mi><mi>A</mi><mi>v</mi><mo>×</mo><mi>v</mi></math> 

Force due to bounce back  <math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>ρ</mi><mi>A</mi><mi>v</mi><mo>×</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>v</mi></math>  

Pressure <math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>ρ</mi><mi>A</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>ρ</mi><mi>A</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi>ρ</mi><msup><mrow><mi>v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

---

### Q77. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

In the following arrangement, the system is initially held at rest by an external agent. At <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>, the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> block is released. If the acceleration of block <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mn>10</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>, then find the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>. Assume that the pulleys and strings are massless and smooth. [<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>9.8</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>]

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image29-72fa48c5-cbb5-436f-9438-c045efef259b.jpg" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **7**

**Detailed Solution:**

Block B will not move

<math><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math> ...(i)

<math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi></math> ...(ii)

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image30-17999e0d-727a-477c-b7ab-68d485245875.jpg" />

<math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>14</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mo>∴</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>14</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>9.8</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>14</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>0.7</mn></math>

<math><mo>∴</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn></math>

</details>

---

### Q78. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> is suspended from a ceiling through a string of length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math>. The particle moves in a horizontal circle of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math>. The speed (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi></math>) of the particle and the tension (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi></math>) in the string is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mtext>2</mtext><mi>r</mi><msqrt><mtext>g</mtext></msqrt></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mtext>1/2&#160;</mtext></msup></mfrac></math>  and  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mtext>3</mtext><mi>m</mi><mtext>g</mtext><mi>L</mi></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mtext>1/2</mtext></msup></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>r</mi><msqrt><mtext>g</mtext></msqrt></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mtext>1/4</mtext></msup></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mtext>g</mtext><mi>L</mi></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mtext>1/3</mtext></msup></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mtext>3</mtext><mi>r</mi><msqrt><mtext>g</mtext></msqrt></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mtext>1/2</mtext></msup></mfrac><mspace width="0.5em"/></math>and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mspace width="0.5em"/><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mtext>g</mtext><mi>L</mi></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mtext>1/2</mtext></msup></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>r</mi><msqrt><mtext>g</mtext></msqrt></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mtext>1/4</mtext></msup></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mtext>g</mtext><mi>L</mi></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mtext>1/2</mtext></msup></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>r</mi><msqrt><mtext>g</mtext></msqrt></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mtext>1/4</mtext></msup></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><mtext>g</mtext><mi>L</mi></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mtext>1/2</mtext></msup></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The situation is shown in the diagram. The angle &theta; made by the string with the vertical is given by

              <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>sin</mi><mfenced><mi>&#952;</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>r</mi><mi>L</mi></mfrac></math>                               ...(i)
 
<strong><u><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0014019_AnsExp4_1.jpg" style="width: 196px; height: 295px;" /></u></strong>

The forces on the particle are

(1) the tension T along the string and

(2) the weight mg vertically downward.

The particle is moving in a circle with constant speed <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi></math>. Thus, the radial acceleration towards the centre has magnitude<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>r</mi></mfrac></math>. Resolving the forces along the radial direction and applying Newton&#39;s second law,

                 <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>sin</mi><mo>&#8203;</mo><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>m</mi><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>r</mi></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo></math>              ...(ii)

As there is no acceleration in the vertical direction, net force in the vertical direction is zero,

             <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cos</mi><mo>&#8203;</mo><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo></math>                    ...(iii)

Dividing (ii) by (iii),

<math> <mrow> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>tan</mtext> <mtext>&#952;</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>v</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>rg</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mn>or</mn> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>v</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <msqrt> <mtext>rg</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>tan</mtext> <mtext>&#952;</mtext> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>

And from (iii),

<math> <mrow> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace><mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>mg</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <mtext>cos &#952;</mtext> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

Using (i),

<math> <mrow> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>v</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>r</mtext> <msqrt> <mtext>g</mtext> </msqrt> </mrow> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>L</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>-</mo> <msup> <mtext>r</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mtext>1/4</mtext> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>and</mtext> <mspace width="0.5em"></mspace> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mtext>mgL</mtext> </mrow> <mrow> <msup> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>L</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mo>-</mo> <msup> <mtext>r</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> <mtext>1/2</mtext> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>​

</details>

---

### Q79. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

A small spherical ball (obeying Stoke's law for viscous force) is thrown up vertically with a speed <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and is received back by the thrower at the point of projection with a speed <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. Neglecting the buoyant force on the ball, assuming the speed of the ball during its flight to be never equal to its terminal speed and taking the acceleration due to gravity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>, find the time of flight of the ball in seconds.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>md</mi><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></mrow><mi>dt</mi></mfrac></math> </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi>dt</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mfrac><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi>dt</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math></p><p>Integrating both sides <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mo>&#8747;</mo><mn>0</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></msubsup><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mi>dt</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mfrac><msubsup><mo>&#8747;</mo><mn>0</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></msubsup><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mi>dt</mi><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mo>&#8747;</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>gT</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q80. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A small ring of mass m is constrained to slide along a horizontal wire fixed between two rigid supports. The ring is connected to a particle of same mass by an ideal string &amp; the whole system is released from rest as shown in the figure. If the coefficient of friction between ring A and wire is <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></math>, the ring will start sliding when the connecting string will make an angle <math> <mrow> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </math> with the vertical, then <math> <mrow> <mi>θ</mi> </mrow> </math> will be (particle is free to move and ring can slide only)

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0067036_solution.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 240px; height: 68px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** 30<sup>o</sup>
- **(B)** 45<sup>o</sup>
- **(C)** 60<sup>o</sup>
- **(D)** None of these

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 45<sup>o</sup>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/EM0067036_Question.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 252px; height: 120px;" /></p><p>Using energy conservation, we get</p><p><math> <mrow> <mo>mgl cos</mo> <mi>&#952;</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mtext>mv</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>mv</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>mgcos&#952;</mi></math></p><p>As the mass is moving in a circular motion, applying circular motion equation, 

<math> <mrow> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mtext>mg cos</mtext> <mi>&#952;</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mtext>mg cos</mtext> <mi>&#952;</mi> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>T</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mtext>mg cos</mtext> <mi>&#952;</mi> </mrow> </math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Tsin&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>mg</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>Tcos&#952;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mtext>mg cos</mtext> <mi>&#952;</mi> <mo>sin</mo> <mi>&#952;</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mi>&#956;</mi> <mfenced close="]" open="[" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>mg</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mtext>mg</mtext> <msup> <mo>cos</mo> <mn>2</mn> </msup> <mi>&#952;</mi> </mrow> </mfenced> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mi>&#952;</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mn>4</mn> <msup> <mn>5</mn> <mo>&#8728;</mo> </msup> </mrow> </math></p>

</details>

---

### Q81. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

An <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>80</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math> person is parachuting and is experiencing a downward acceleration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2.8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>. The mass of the parachute is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kg</mi></math>. If the upward force on the open parachute is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>, then what is the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi></math>? (Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>9.8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **5.95**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Mass of the person <math><mi>M</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>80</mn></math> kg

Mass of the parachute <math><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math> kg

<math><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> Total mass of the system <math><mo>=</mo><mi>M</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>85</mn></math>kg

Downward acceleration <math><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2.8</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

Let upward force <math><mo>=</mo><mi>F</mi></math>

Applying Newton&#39;s II law of motion to this system

<math><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>g</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>a</mi></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>g</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>85</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>9.8</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2.8</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>

<math><mo>=</mo><mn>85</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>7</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>595</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>95</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>95</mn></math></p><p> </p>

</details>

---

### Q82. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A body takes time <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi></math> to reach the bottom of an inclined plane of angle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>θ</mi></math><i> </i>with the horizontal. If the plane is made rough, time taken now is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>t</mi></math>. The coefficient of friction of the rough surface is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mn>3</mn> <mn>4</mn> </mfrac> <mi>tan</mi><mi>θ</mi></mrow>  </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mn>2</mn> <mn>3</mn> </mfrac> <mi>tan</mi><mi>θ</mi></mrow>  </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mn>4</mn> </mfrac> <mi>tan</mi><mi>θ</mi></mrow>  </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> <mi>tan</mi><mi>θ</mi></mrow>  </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mn>3</mn> <mn>4</mn> </mfrac> <mi>tan</mi><mi>θ</mi></mrow>  </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <mi>μ</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>tan</mi><mi>θ</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mn>1</mn><mo>−</mo><mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mrow> <msup> <mi>n</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow>  </math>  <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo><mi>tan</mi><mi>θ</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mn>1</mn><mo>−</mo><mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mrow> <msup> <mn>2</mn> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow><mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mn>3</mn> <mn>4</mn> </mfrac> <mi>tan</mi><mi>θ</mi></mrow>  </math>

</details>

---

### Q83. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A particle is projected directly along a rough plane of inclination <math><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></math> with velocity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></math>. If after coming to the rest the particle returns to the starting point with velocity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></math>, the coefficient of friction between the particle and the plane is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac><mi>tan&#952;</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>–</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mi>tanθ</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac><mi>tan&#952;</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#8211;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mi>tan&#952;</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>–</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mi>tanθ</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>2</mn><mi>as</mi></math>

For upward motion <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mi>&#956;gcos&#952;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>gsin&#952;</mi><mo>)</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>

For downward motion <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mi>gsin&#952;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#956;gcos&#952;</mi><mo>)</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#956;cos&#952;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>sin&#952;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>sin&#952;</mi><mo>&#8211;</mo><mi>&#956;cos&#952;</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>sin&#952;</mi><mo>-</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>&#956;cos&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>&#956;v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>cos&#952;</mi><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>sin&#952;</mi></math> 

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>)</mo><mi>sin&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>(</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>)</mo><mi>cos&#952;</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#8211;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mi>tan&#952;</mi></math>

</details>

---

### Q84. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A block is kept on a smooth inclined plane of the angle of inclination <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo>°</mo></math> that moves with a constant acceleration so that the block does not slide relative to the inclined plane. Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> be the contact force between the block and the plane. Now the inclined plane stops and let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> be the contact force between the two in this case. Then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>F</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

When block does not slide with respect to the inclined plane,

<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo></math> normal reaction <math><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mo>&#8757;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">tan</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mo>&#8756;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">tan</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>sec</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>sec</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math>

When incline plane is stationary,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>cos</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi>cos</mi><mo>&#8289;</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="italic">mg</mi></math>

<math><mo>&#8756;</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

---

### Q85. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A ball is held at rest at position <math><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> by two light strings. The horizontal string is cut and the ball starts swinging like a pendulum. Point <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> is the farthest to the right the ball goes as it swings back and forth. What is the ratio of the tension in the supporting string in position <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> to its value at <math><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> before the horizontal string was cut?

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image28-15b3fcf9-4af7-4ccd-9b0f-0f8b25a9d029.jpg" style="height: 179px; width: 180px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">β</mi></mrow></mrow></math>
- **(B)** <math><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">β</mi></mrow></mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">tan</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">β</mi></mrow></mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">β</mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">β</mi></mrow></mrow></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image28-15b3fcf9-4af7-4ccd-9b0f-0f8b25a9d029.jpg" style="height: 179px; width: 180px;" />

At A:

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image29-e4c7f6e2-d413-4386-bab6-2ee5cab1d267.jpg" style="height: 158px; width: 180px;" /></p><p>
<math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi></mrow></msup><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">β</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">β</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfrac></math>

At: B


<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image30-79e33114-2f7f-4979-bbb9-4ceca2ffa8fb.jpg" style="height: 147px; width: 180px;" />

<math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">β</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

at <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">β</mi></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">β</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">β</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">′</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">β</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q86. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A conveyor belt is moving at a constant speed of  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mtext>m </mtext><msup><mtext>s</mtext><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.  A box is gently dropped on it. The coefficient of friction between them is  <math> <mrow> <mi>μ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.5.</mn> </mrow> </math>  The distance that the box will move relative to belt before coming to rest on it taking  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mtext> </mtext><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(C)** Zero
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p align="left" style="text-align:left;"><span>The frictional force on the box  <math> <mrow> <mi>f</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi> </mrow> </math> </span></p><p align="left" style="text-align:left;"><span style="position:relative;top:0.0pt"> <math> <mo>&#8756;</mo> </math> </span><span>  Acceleration in the box</span></p><p align="left" style="text-align:left;"><span>                <math> <mrow> <mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi>m</mi><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><msup> <mi>s</mi> <mrow> <mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>2</mn> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math> </span></p><math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi><mi>s</mi></math>

<math><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>s</mn></math>

<mml:math xmlns:m="http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/officeDocument/2006/math" xmlns:mml="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mml:mn></mml:mn><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>s</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math><mml:mi></mml:mi></mml:math>

</details>

---

### Q87. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Two masses <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi></math> are attached with strings as shown in the figure. For the system to be in equilibrium, we must have

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/26516.JPG" style="max-width:"100%"; object-fit:contain" style="width: 150px; height: 170px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>tan</mtext><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>M</mi></mrow><mi>m</mi></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>tan</mtext><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow><mi>M</mi></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>tan</mtext><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>M</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>tan</mtext><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>m</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>tan</mtext><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>M</mi></mrow><mi>m</mi></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>T</mi><mi>sin</mi><mn>45</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi>T</mi></math>

  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mtext>&#160;cos</mtext><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>cos</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mn>5</mn><mo>&#8728;</mo></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mtext>cos</mtext><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>T</mi><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">mg</mtext><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>              ...(i)

Further, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext mathvariant="italic">Mg</mtext><mo>+</mo><mi>T</mi><mtext>&#160;cos&#160;</mtext><mn>4</mn><msup><mn>5</mn><mo>&#8728;</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><msub><mtext mathvariant="italic">T</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub><mtext>&#160;sin</mtext><mi>&#952;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mi><mfenced open="{" close="}"><mrow><mo>&#8757;</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">T</mtext><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">mg</mtext><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mtext>&#160;sin</mtext><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mtext mathvariant="italic">Mg</mtext><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">mg</mtext><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math>              ...(ii)

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo><mtext>tan</mtext><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mtext mathvariant="italic">Mg</mtext><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">mg</mtext><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow><mfrac><mtext mathvariant="italic">mg</mtext><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mtext mathvariant="italic">M</mtext></mrow><mtext mathvariant="italic">m</mtext></mfrac></math></p><p> {dividing Eq. (ii) by Eq. (i)}</p>

</details>

### Q88. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

When a block moves down a smooth inclined plane of inclination <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>θ</mi></math>, its velocity on reaching the bottom is <math><mi>v</mi></math>. When it slides down a rough inclined plane of same inclination, its velocity on reaching the bottom is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>n</mi></math>, where <math><mi>n</mi></math> is a number greater than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>. The coefficient of friction between the block and the rough surface is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>μ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mi>tan</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi>θ</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#x3BC;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mi>cot</mi><mo>&#x2061;</mo><mi>&#x3B8;</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#x3BC;</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mfrac bevelled="true"><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup><mi>tan</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">&#x3B8;</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#x3BC;</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mfrac bevelled="true"><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup><mi>cot</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">&#x3B8;</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>μ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mi>tan</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi>θ</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>For smooth incline, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>v</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>g</mi><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>s</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

For rough incline, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mi>v</mi><mi>n</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>g</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>s</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>On solving (1) and (2), we obtain,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mi>tan</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q89. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Initially, both the blocks are at rest on horizontal surface as shown in the figure. Find the minimum value of force <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi></math> in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></mfenced></math> so that sliding starts between the blocks <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/dlgwtzwyzmbbtwrbhenhnmebeqzncjalqhkvktmmqldpzgdmog.jpeg" /></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **80**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/dlazsyhvzjonrrmjdpttlyjztuaogesorniskuzmxbumdjsydo.jpeg" />

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>20</mn><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>a</mi></math> .......(i)

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>10</mn><mi>g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>a</mi></math> ..................(ii)</p><p>From (i) and (ii)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>g</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>80</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q90. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A block of mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> is pulled by a uniform chain of the same mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math> tied to it by applying a force <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi></math> at the other end of the chain. The tension at the point which is at a distance of a quarter of the length of the chain from the end on which the force is applied, will be

<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/6435254.png" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 396px; height: 114px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="italic">F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><mi mathvariant="italic">F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="italic">F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>N</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>x</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>f</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>c</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>c</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>f</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>n</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>T</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>t</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>m</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>s</mi><mi>&#160;</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>F</mi><mrow><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>F</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><msub><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mi>m</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mi>F</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q91. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Three blocks are arranged on a horizontal table <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ABCD</mi></math> as shown in the figure. The strings and pulleys are massless and both the pulleys stand vertical. The strings connecting blocks <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></math> and <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> are also vertical and are perpendicular to faces <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AB</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>BC</mi></math> which are mutually perpendicular to each other. If <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></math> and <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math> respectively. Coefficient of friction between the block <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kg</mi></math>  and the surface is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.6</mn></math> then, frictional force on <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub></math> is


<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image20-3545de18-a755-4fd6-b33a-b608c56c49ed.jpg" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Net force on <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>30</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>40</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>50</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>

And limiting friction on <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>60</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>

<math><mo>∴</mo></math> System remain in equilibrium and friction on <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>50</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>

</details>

### Q92. NTA Abhyas (JEE Main) 2020

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

What is the maximum value of the force <math><mi>F</mi></math> (in newton) such that the block shown in the arrangement, does not move?


<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/force-image15-a2864999-fea4-4300-a767-540edb3fe5c5.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 272px; height: 130px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **20**

**Detailed Solution:**

Free body diagram (FBD) of the block (shown by a dot) is shown in figure.


<img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/nta_abhyas/jee_main/images/ci/image45-082ba680-de94-4eb3-8d69-c5a73ec764a5.jpg" style="max-width:100%; object-fit:contain" style="width: 388px; height: 151px;" />

For vertical equilibrium of the block,

<math><mi>N</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>g</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>F</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mn>60</mn><mo>°</mo><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mrow><mi>g</mi><mo>+</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi> </mi><mo>…</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

For no motion, force of friction

<math><mi>f</mi><mo>≥</mo><mi>F</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mn>60</mn><mo>°</mo></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mo>°</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><mi> </mi><mi>N</mi><mo>≥</mo><mi>F</mi><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">cos</mi></mrow><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mn>60</mn><mo>°</mo></mrow></mrow></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi>g</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi> </mi><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>≥</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi>g</mi><mo>≥</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>F</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi>F</mi><mo>≤</mo><mn>2</mn><mi> </mi><mi>g</mi><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mn>20</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>

Therefore, maximum value of F is 20 N.

</details>
